5.47
). Note that the number of root clusters in
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}
does not exceed
s=Q_{\ell}\leq A
. By Definition
5.7
(
3
) and Definition
5.11
, we see that the number of particle lines in
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}
does not exceed
\Lambda_{\ell}^{2}A_{\ell}
. Thus, we may insert (
5.40
) into the second line of (
5.49
), and use the definitions (
5.42
), to get

(5.51)

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell},\mathrm{upp}}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{Q_{\ell}})

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-(d-1)|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}|_{p\backslash r}}\int|(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}||\widetilde{f}_{|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}|_{p}}((\ell-1)\tau,\bm{z}_{p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})}^{\prime})|\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{(p\backslash r)(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{H_{\ell-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})}|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell},H_{\ell-1}}|(\bm{z}_{Q_{\ell}})+\mathrm{Err}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}^{2}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{Q_{\ell}}),

where
\mathrm{Err}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}^{2}
satisfies that

(5.52)

|\mathrm{Err}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}^{2}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{Q_{\ell}})|\leq\varepsilon^{-(d-1)|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}|_{p\backslash r}}\int|(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}||\mathrm{Err}((\ell-1)\tau,\bm{z}_{p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})}^{\prime})|\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{(p\backslash r)(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})}.

Inserting (
5.51
)–(
5.52
) into the first line of (
5.49
), we get

(5.53)

|\textrm{Err}^{0}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{Q_{\ell}})|\leq\sum_{[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{err}},\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})=Q_{\ell},\,H_{\ell-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})}|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell},H_{\ell-1}}|(\bm{z}_{Q_{\ell}})+|\textrm{Err}^{2,2}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{Q_{\ell}})|,

where

(5.54)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell},H_{\ell-1}}|(\bm{z}_{Q_{\ell}})

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-(d-1)|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}|_{p\backslash r}}\int|(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}|\left(|f^{\mathcal{A}}((\ell-1)\tau)|^{\otimes(p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})\backslash H_{\ell-1})}\cdot|E_{H_{\ell-1}}((\ell-1)\tau)|\right),

\displaystyle|\textrm{Err}^{2,2}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{Q_{\ell}})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{err}},\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})=Q_{\ell}}\varepsilon^{-(d-1)|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}|_{p\backslash r}}\int|(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}||\mathrm{Err}((\ell-1)\tau,\bm{z}_{p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})})|\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{(p\backslash r)(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})}.

Now we define the
\sum(\cdots)
term on the right hand side of (
5.53
) to be
\mathrm{Err}^{1}
, then (
5.45
) follows immediately from this definition. As for the term
\mathrm{Err}^{2,2}
, we shall combine it with the term
\mathrm{Err}^{2,1}
below (which comes from
\mathrm{Main}^{0}
in (
5.47
)) to form
\mathrm{Err}^{2}
, and prove it satisfies (
5.46
), which would then complete the proof of (
3
).

Now consider
\mathrm{Main}^{0}
in (
5.47
). By inserting (
5.40
) into the second line of (
5.48
), and using the definitions (
5.42
), we get

(5.55)

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{Q_{\ell}})

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-(d-1)|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}|_{p\backslash r}}\int(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})^{\mathrm{Pen}}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}\widetilde{f}_{|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}|_{p}}((\ell-1)\tau,\bm{z}_{p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})}^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{(p\backslash r)(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{H_{\ell-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})}\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell},H_{\ell-1}}^{\mathrm{Pen}}(\bm{z}_{Q_{\ell}})+\mathrm{Err}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}^{2}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{Q_{\ell}}),

where
\mathrm{Err}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}^{2}
satisfies the same bound (
5.52
).
Inserting (
5.55
) into the first line of (
5.48
), we get

(5.56)

\textrm{Main}^{0}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{Q_{\ell}})=\sum_{[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}},\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})=Q_{\ell},\,H_{\ell-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})}\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell},H_{\ell-1}}^{\mathrm{Pen}}(\bm{z}_{Q_{\ell}})+\mathrm{Err}^{2,1}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{Q_{\ell}}),

where

(5.57)

\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell},H_{\ell-1}}^{\mathrm{Pen}}(\bm{z}_{Q_{\ell}})=\varepsilon^{-(d-1)|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}|_{p\backslash r}}\int(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})^{\mathrm{Pen}}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}\left((f^{\mathcal{A}}((\ell-1)\tau))^{\otimes(p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})\backslash H_{\ell-1})}\cdot E_{H_{\ell-1}}((\ell-1)\tau)\right),

and
\mathrm{Err}^{2,1}
is the same as
\mathrm{Err}^{2,2}
in (
5.54
) but with summation taken over
[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}
. By combining this with
\mathrm{Err}^{2,2}
we get the term
\mathrm{Err}^{2}
, which the same as in (
5.54
) but with summation taken over
[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{err}}
.

Next we prove (
5.46
) for
\mathrm{Err}^{2}
. Note that
|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}|_{p\backslash r}\leq\Lambda_{\ell}^{2}A_{\ell}
as proved before. Using
L^{1}
conservation, it is easy to see that the
L^{1}
norm of each individual term in the summation on the right hand side of (
5.54
) is bounded by
\varepsilon^{-(d-1)\Lambda_{\ell}^{2}A_{\ell}}\cdot\|\mathrm{Err}((\ell-1)\tau)\|_{L^{1}}
. Moreover, consider all possible choices of labeled molecules
[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{err}}
(equivalently
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}\in\mathscr{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}\cup\mathscr{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{err}}
). Since the total number of particles is
\leq\Lambda_{\ell}^{2}A_{\ell}\leq|\log\varepsilon|^{C^{*}}
, and each cluster has at most
\Gamma
recollisions due to truncation, we may simply fix all the collisions and overlaps and their relative ordering, and the combinatorial factor caused by this is bounded by
(|\log\varepsilon|^{C^{*}})^{\Lambda_{\ell}^{2}A_{\ell}}
. This proves (
5.46
) and finishes the proof of (
3
).

Proof part 2.
It remains to construct the expression (
5.50
) and prove (
1
)–(
2
). This will come from the
\sum(\cdots)
term in (
5.56
). Note that this involves a molecule
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}
and a set
H_{\ell-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})
. Consider the components of
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}
. We say a component is
single
, if it contains a unique root particle
r\in r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})
(we also say this
r
is
single
), and does not contain any particle in
H_{\ell-1}
.

Note that the single components are labeled by single root particles
r
, which we denote by
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)
. Also define
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}
to be the union of all
non-single
components of
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}
, and denote
r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})=H_{\ell}\subseteq Q_{\ell}
to be the set of non-single particles. By definition of single, it is easy to see that
r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})\searrow H_{\ell-1}
. See
Figure
10
.

Figure 10.
The molecule
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}
and its single component
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)
, and non-single component
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}
. Here the particle lines in
r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})=Q_{\ell}
are marked as red, and those in
H_{\ell-1}
marked as green. In particular,
H_{\ell}=r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})
is formed by the 3 red particle lines in
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}
.

Note that, as
unlabeled
molecules,
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}
is in one-to-one correspondence with
(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r))_{r\not\in H_{\ell}}
and
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}
. Next we show that

(5.58)

\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell},H_{\ell-1}}^{\mathrm{Pen}}(\bm{z}_{Q_{\ell}})=\bigg(\prod_{r\notin H_{\ell}}\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r),\varnothing}^{\mathrm{Pen}}(z_{r})\bigg)\cdot\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime},H_{\ell-1}}^{\mathrm{Pen}}(\bm{z}_{H_{\ell}}).

To prove this, simply note that

(5.59)

(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{Pen}}=\prod_{r\notin H_{\ell}}(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)}^{\mathrm{Pen}}\circ(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Pen}}

by (
5.14
). Inserting this into the the first line of (
5.42
), we get

(5.60)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell},H_{\ell-1}}^{\mathrm{Pen}}(\bm{z}_{Q_{\ell}})

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-(d-1)|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}|_{p\backslash r}}\int(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})^{\mathrm{Pen}}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}\left((f^{\mathcal{A}}((\ell-1)\tau))^{\otimes(p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})\backslash H_{\ell-1})}\cdot E_{H_{\ell-1}}((\ell-1)\tau)\right)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-(d-1)|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}|_{p\backslash r}}\int\prod_{r\notin H_{\ell}}\left((\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)}^{\mathrm{Pen}}(f^{\mathcal{A}}((\ell-1)\tau))^{\otimes(p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)))}\right)\times

\displaystyle\int(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{Pen}}\left((f^{\mathcal{A}}((\ell-1)\tau))^{\otimes(p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})\backslash H_{\ell-1})}\cdot E_{H_{\ell-1}}((\ell-1)\tau)\right)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\prod_{r\notin H_{\ell}}\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r),\varnothing}^{\mathrm{Pen}}(z_{r})\bigg)\cdot\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime},H_{\ell-1}}^{\mathrm{Pen}}(\bm{z}_{H_{\ell}})

which proves (
5.58
).

Now, note that the right hand side of (
5.58
) has the structure

\prod_{r\not\in H_{\ell}}\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r))\cdot\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime},H_{\ell-1}),

where
\mathrm{Fun}(\cdot)
is an arbitrary expression depending only on the object
(\cdot)
. We claim that using the convention in Definition
1.6
(
6
), we have

(5.61)

\displaystyle\sum_{[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}},\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})=Q_{\ell},\,H_{\ell-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})}\prod_{r\not\in H_{\ell}}\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r))\cdot\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime},H_{\ell-1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{H_{\ell}\subseteq Q_{\ell}}\bigg(\prod_{r\notin H_{\ell}}\sum_{[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)]\in\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}}\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r))\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}},\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})=H_{\ell}\\
H_{\ell-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}),\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})\searrow H_{\ell-1}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime},H_{\ell-1}).

Note that this equality is not trivial (even though
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}
is in one-to-one correspondence with
(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r))_{r\not\in H_{\ell}}
and
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}
), because we need to account for labelings of these molecules, and in general
\mathrm{Fun}
is not constant on equivalence classes.

The proof of (
5.61
) involves some complicated calculation of multinomial coefficients, and will be left to Part 4 below. For now we use it to finish the proof of Proposition
5.16
. Indeed, by inserting (
5.58
) and (
5.61
) into the first line of (
5.56
), we get

(5.62)

\displaystyle\textrm{$\sum(\cdots)$ in (\ref{eq.proof_cumulant_single_1_2})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}},\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})=Q_{\ell},\,H_{\ell-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})}\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell},H_{\ell-1}}^{\mathrm{Pen}}(\bm{z}_{Q_{\ell}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{H_{\ell}\subseteq Q_{\ell}}\bigg(\prod_{r\notin H_{\ell}}\sum_{[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)]\in\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}}\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r),\varnothing}^{\mathrm{Pen}}(z_{r})\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}},\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})=H_{\ell},\\
H_{\ell-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}),\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})\searrow H_{\ell-1}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime},H_{\ell-1}}^{\mathrm{Pen}}(\bm{z}_{H_{\ell}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{H_{\ell}\subseteq Q_{\ell}}(f^{\mathcal{A}}(\ell\tau))^{\otimes(Q_{\ell}\backslash H_{\ell})}\cdot E_{H_{\ell}}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{H_{\ell}}),

where

(5.63)

\displaystyle f^{\mathcal{A}}(\ell\tau)

\displaystyle=\sum_{[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)]\in\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}}\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r),\varnothing}^{\mathrm{Pen}}(z_{r})

\displaystyle E_{H_{\ell}}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{H_{\ell}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}},\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})=H_{\ell}\\
H_{\ell-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}),\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})\searrow H_{\ell-1}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime},H_{\ell-1}}^{\mathrm{Pen}}(\bm{z}_{H_{\ell}}).

This proves (
5.50
), together with the formulas (
5.43
)–(
5.44
) in (
1
)–(
2
) (note that trivially
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M},H}^{\mathrm{Pen}}\leq|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M},H}|
). Therefore, the proof of Proposition
5.16
is complete, pending the proof of (
5.61
).

Proof part 4.
Finally we prove (
5.61
). Without loss of generality, we assume that
r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})=Q_{\ell}=[s]
. In the expressions below, for convenience of writing, we will replace
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r))
by
\bigcirc_{r}
, and
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime},H_{\ell-1})
by
\bigcirc^{\prime}
, and their product (in the first line of (
5.61
)) by
\bigcirc
. By (
5.34
), we have

(5.64)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}},\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})=[s]\\
H_{\ell-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})\end{subarray}}\bigcirc

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbb{M}_{\ell}\in\mathscr{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}},\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})=[s]\\
H_{\ell-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}),\,p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})=[|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}|_{p}]\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{(|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}|_{p}-s)!}\cdot\bigcirc

\displaystyle=\sum_{n_{\ell}=s}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(n_{\ell}-s)!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbb{M}_{\ell}\in\mathscr{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}},\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})=[s]\\
H_{\ell-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}),\,p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})=[n_{\ell}]\end{subarray}}\bigcirc

\displaystyle=\sum_{n_{\ell}=s}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(n_{\ell}-s)!}\sum_{H_{\ell}\subseteq[s]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r))_{r\not\in H_{\ell}}\\
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)\in\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda_{\ell}},\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r))=\{r\}\end{subarray}}\prod_{r}\bigcirc_{r}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}},H_{\ell-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})\\
p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})=[n_{\ell}],\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})=H_{\ell}\\
r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})\searrow H_{\ell-1}\end{subarray}}\bigcirc^{\prime}.

By Definition
5.7
(
2c
), we know that the summand
\bigcirc
remains invariant under any relabeling that preserves the order of labels within each component. Now consider the summation in (
5.64
) with
n_{\ell}
,
H_{\ell}
and
|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}|_{p\backslash r}:=m^{\prime}
fixed, and with
|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)|_{p\backslash r}:=m_{r}
fixed for each
r
. The number of choices for the
non-root particle sets
of each
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)
and
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}
is given by the multinomial coefficient
\binom{n_{\ell}-s}{m_{1},\cdots,m_{r},m^{\prime}}
. For each fixed choice of
these sets
, we have

(5.65)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r))_{r\not\in H_{\ell}}:\,p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r))\,\textrm{fixed}\\
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)\in\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda_{\ell}},\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r))=\{r\}\end{subarray}}\prod_{r}\bigcirc_{r}=\prod_{r\not\in H_{\ell}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)\in\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}:\,p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r))\,\textrm{fixed}\\
r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r))=\{r\}\end{subarray}}\bigcirc_{r}=\prod_{r\not\in H_{\ell}}m_{r}!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)]\in\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}:|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)|_{p\backslash r}=m_{r}\\
r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r))=\{r\}\end{subarray}}\bigcirc_{r},

where the last equality is because different choices of
p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r))
do not affect the expression
\bigcirc_{r}
or the average in the equivalence class
[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)]
by the above invariance property. Similarly we have

(5.66)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}},H_{\ell-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})\\
p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})\,\textrm{fixed},\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})=H_{\ell}\\
r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})\searrow H_{\ell-1}\end{subarray}}\bigcirc^{\prime}=(m^{\prime})!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}:|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}|_{p\backslash r}=m^{\prime},\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})=H_{\ell}\\
H_{\ell-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}),\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})\searrow H_{\ell-1}\end{subarray}}\bigcirc^{\prime}.

By inserting (
5.65
)–(
5.66
) into (
5.64
), and first summing in
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)
and
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}
and then in the non-root particle sets and finally in
m_{r}
and
m^{\prime}
, we get

(5.67)

\displaystyle(\ref{eq.proof_cumulant_single_4_1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{n_{\ell}=s}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(n_{\ell}-s)!}\sum_{H_{\ell}\subseteq[s]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(m_{r})_{r\not\in H_{\ell}},m^{\prime}\\
m^{\prime}+\sum_{r}m_{r}=n_{\ell}-s\end{subarray}}\binom{n_{\ell}-s}{m_{1},\cdots,m_{r},m^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{r\not\in H_{\ell}}m_{r}!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)]\in\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}:|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)|_{p\backslash r}=m_{r}\\
r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r))=\{r\}\end{subarray}}\bigcirc_{r}\cdot(m^{\prime})!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}:|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}|_{p\backslash r}=m^{\prime},\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})=H_{\ell}\\
H_{\ell-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}),\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})\searrow H_{\ell-1}\end{subarray}}\bigcirc^{\prime}.

Using the multinomial formula, we see that the combinatorial factors in (
5.67
) exactly cancel. Let the summations in the second line of (
5.67
) be
X_{m_{r}}
and
Y_{m^{\prime}}
respectively, then

(5.68)

\displaystyle(\ref{eq.proof_cumulant_single_4_1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{H_{\ell}\subseteq[s]}\sum_{n_{\ell}=s}^{\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(m_{r})_{r\not\in H_{\ell}},m^{\prime}\\
m^{\prime}+\sum_{r}m_{r}=n_{\ell}-s\end{subarray}}\prod_{r\not\in H_{\ell}}X_{m_{r}}\cdot Y_{m^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{H_{\ell}\subseteq[s]}\sum_{(m_{r})_{r\not\in H_{\ell}},m^{\prime}}\prod_{r\not\in H_{\ell}}X_{m_{r}}\cdot Y_{m^{\prime}}=\sum_{H_{\ell}\subseteq[s]}\prod_{r\not\in H_{\ell}}\bigg(\sum_{m_{r}}X_{m_{r}}\bigg)\cdot\bigg(\sum_{m^{\prime}}Y_{m^{\prime}}\bigg)

where we have changed the order of summation. Finally, by definition of
X_{m_{r}}
and
Y_{m^{\prime}}
we have

(5.69)

\sum_{m_{r}}X_{m_{r}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)]\in\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}\\
r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r))=\{r\}\end{subarray}}\bigcirc_{r}=\sum_{[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)]\in\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}}\bigcirc_{r},\qquad\sum_{m^{\prime}}Y_{m^{\prime}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}},\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})=H_{\ell}\\
H_{\ell-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime}),\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}^{\prime})\searrow H_{\ell-1}\end{subarray}}\bigcirc^{\prime}

(note that
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}(r)
has only one root particle, and renaming it into
r
or anything else does not affect the expression
\bigcirc_{r}
or the average in the equivalence class). By putting together (
5.68
) and (
5.69
), we have proved (
5.61
). This completes the proof of Proposition
5.16
.
∎

5.5.
The initial cumulant expansion

In this subsection we prove the initial cumulant expansion (
5.41
) for
\ell=0
, without the
\mathrm{Err}
term, see Proposition
5.18
. The initial cumulants
E_{H_{0}}(0)
correspond to the closeness scenarios
|x_{p_{1}}-x_{p_{2}}|\leq\varepsilon
which, stem from the
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{N}}
indicator functions in (
1.7
).

Proposition 5.18

(Initial cumulant expansion)
.

Assume that the initial density function
W_{0,N}(\bm{z}_{N})
is given by (
1.7
). Then for each set
Q_{0}
with
|Q_{0}|\leq A_{0}
(
A_{0}
is defined in (
1.32
)), we have initial cumulant expansion at
t=0
:

(5.70)

f(0,\bm{z}_{Q_{0}})=\sum_{H_{0}\subseteq Q_{0}}(f^{\mathcal{A}}(0))^{\otimes(Q_{0}\backslash H_{0})}\cdot E_{H_{0}}(0,\bm{z}_{H_{0}}),

where
f^{\mathcal{A}}(0,z)=f_{0}(z)
and
E_{H_{0}}(0,\bm{z}_{H_{0}})
satisfies the following upper bound

(5.71)

|E_{H_{0}}(0,\bm{z}_{H_{0}})|\leq(f^{\mathcal{A}}(0))^{\otimes H_{0}}\sum_{\mathcal{L}:p(\mathcal{L})=H_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\varepsilon}.

Here
\mathcal{L}
is a collection of pairs
(p_{1},p_{2})
with
p_{1},p_{2}\in H_{0}
(cf. Definition
3.1
(
3
), where bottom ends are identified with particle lines). This
\mathcal{L}
is a forest with no isolated vertex, when viewed as a graph over vertex set
H_{0}
. Moreover,
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\varepsilon}
is defined by

(5.72)

\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\varepsilon}=\prod_{(p_{1},p_{2})\in\mathcal{L}}\mathbbm{1}_{p_{1}\sim_{\mathrm{in}}p_{2}}^{\varepsilon}\qquad\textrm{and}\qquad\mathbbm{1}_{p_{1}\sim_{\mathrm{in}}p_{2}}^{\varepsilon}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1\quad&&|x_{p_{1}}-x_{p_{2}}|\leq\varepsilon,\\
&\varepsilon^{\upsilon}\quad&&|x_{p_{1}}-x_{p_{2}}|\geq\varepsilon.\end{aligned}\right.

where
\upsilon=3^{-d-1}
, as in (
3.9
).

Proof.

The following proof is an adaptation of the proof of
[
49
]
, Proposition A.1. To start with, we introduce the following notations:

(5.73)

\mathbbm{1}_{A\not\sim B}=\mathbbm{1}_{\{|z_{p}-z_{p^{\prime}}|\geq\varepsilon,\ \forall p\in A\textrm{ and }p\neq p^{\prime}\in B\}}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathbbm{1}_{p\sim B}=\mathbbm{1}_{\{\exists p^{\prime}\in B,\textrm{ such that }|z_{p}-z_{p^{\prime}}|\leq\varepsilon\}}.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
Q_{0}=[s]
. By (
1.7
), (
1.8
) and (
1.11
), we have

(5.74)

\displaystyle f(0,\bm{z}_{s})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathcal{Z}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\varepsilon^{-(d-1)n}}{n!}\int_{\mathcal{D}_{s+n}}(f^{\mathcal{A}}(0))^{\otimes[s+n]}(\bm{z}_{s+n})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{[s+1:s+n]},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}

\displaystyle=1+\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}\frac{\varepsilon^{-(d-1)N}}{N!}\int_{\mathcal{D}_{N}}(f^{\mathcal{A}}(0))^{\otimes[N]}(\bm{z}_{N})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{N},

which implies that

(5.75)

f(0,\bm{z}_{s})=(f^{\mathcal{A}}(0))^{\otimes[s]}\mathbbm{1}_{[s]\not\sim[s]}\cdot\frac{1}{\mathcal{Z}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\varepsilon^{-(d-1)n}}{n!}\int_{\mathcal{D}_{[s+1:s+n]}}\mathbbm{1}_{[s]\not\sim[s+1:s+n]}(f^{\mathcal{A}}(0))^{\otimes[s+1:s+n]}\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{[s+1:s+n]}.

Note that

(5.76)

\mathbbm{1}_{[s]\not\sim[s+1:s+n]}=\prod_{p\in[s]}\mathbbm{1}_{p\not\sim[s+1:s+n]}=\sum_{H_{1}\subseteq[s]}(-1)^{|H_{1}|}\cdot\prod_{p\in H_{1}}\mathbbm{1}_{p\sim[s+1:s+n]}.

We will fix
H_{1}
and define
M=[s]\backslash H_{1}
, then put aside the factors
\mathbbm{1}_{H_{1}\not\sim H_{1}}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{H_{1}\not\sim M}
that occur in the
\mathbbm{1}_{[s]\not\sim[s]}
in (
5.75
), and focus on
\mathbbm{1}_{M\not\sim M}
. Here we apply a Penrose type argument in the setting of Part 2 of the proof of Proposition
5.10
, by setting

•

E=\{(p,p^{\prime}):p\neq p^{\prime}\in M\}
, so subsets of
E
are identified with graphs over vertex set
M
, and denote
\mathbbm{1}_{A_{\mathfrak{o}}}:=\mathbbm{1}_{p\sim p^{\prime}}
if
\mathfrak{o}=(p,p^{\prime})
.

•

There is no
\mathscr{F}^{\mathrm{err}}
, and
\mathscr{F}
is the set of
F\subseteq E
whose corresponding graph are forests;

•

The mapping
\pi:2^{E}\to\mathscr{F}
is any algorithm of generating a spanning forest
F=\pi(G)
from a graph
G
. For example, we can fix a linear ordering of elements of
E
based on labels of particles, then apply the same procedure that is applied to the graph
\mathrm{Cl}_{G}
in (
3
) in the definition of the algorithm in Part 4 of the proof of Proposition
5.10
.

It is easy to see, as in Part 4 of the proof of Proposition
5.10
, that (
5.20
) is true for the above-defined
\mathscr{F}
and
\pi
. Then we get

(5.77)

\mathbbm{1}_{M\not\sim M}=\prod_{\mathfrak{o}\in E}(1-\mathbbm{1}_{A_{\mathfrak{o}}})=\sum_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}(-1)^{|\mathcal{L}_{2}|}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{\mathrm{Pen}},

where
\mathcal{L}_{2}
is a graph over vertex set
M=[s]\backslash H_{1}
that is a forest (corresponding to
F\in\mathscr{F}
above),
|\mathcal{L}_{2}|=|F|
is the number of edges in the graph
\mathcal{L}_{2}
,
p(\mathcal{L}_{2})
is the set of non-isolated vertices in
\mathcal{L}_{2}
, and

\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{\mathrm{Pen}}:=\prod_{(p,p^{\prime})\in\mathcal{L}_{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{p\sim p^{\prime}}\prod_{(p,p^{\prime})\in AL(F)}\mathbbm{1}_{p\not\sim p^{\prime}};\qquad\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{\mathrm{Pen}}\leq\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}:=\prod_{(p,p^{\prime})\in\mathcal{L}_{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{p\sim p^{\prime}}.

Inserting (
5.76
) into (
5.75
), and letting
H_{1}\cup p(\mathcal{L}_{2})=H_{0}
, we get

(5.78)

\displaystyle f(0,\bm{z}_{s})

\displaystyle=\sum_{H_{0}\subseteq[s]}(f^{\mathcal{A}}(0))^{\otimes([s]\backslash H_{0})}\cdot E_{H}(0,\bm{z}_{H_{0}}),

\displaystyle E_{H}(0,\bm{z}_{H_{0}})

\displaystyle=(f^{\mathcal{A}}(0))^{\otimes H}\frac{1}{\mathcal{Z}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\varepsilon^{-(d-1)n}}{n!}\sum_{(H_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2}):H_{1}\cup p(\mathcal{L}_{2})=H_{0}}(-1)^{|\mathcal{L}_{2}|+|H_{1}|}\mathbbm{1}_{H_{1}\not\sim H_{1}}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{H_{1}\not\sim([s]\backslash H_{1})}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{\mathrm{Pen}}

(5.79)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathcal{D}_{[s+1:s+n]}}\prod_{p\in H_{1}}\mathbbm{1}_{p\sim[s+1:s+n]}\cdot(f^{\mathcal{A}}(0))^{\otimes[s+1:s+n]}\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{[s+1:s+n]}.

Recall
\upsilon=3^{-d-1}
. Let
K\subseteq[s+1:s+n]
be the set of
p^{\prime}
such that
p^{\prime}\sim p
for some
p\in H_{1}
. Since each
p^{\prime}
corresponds to at most
(3\upsilon)^{-1}=3^{d}
different
p
(the number of non-intersecting unit balls that all intersect a given unit ball cannot exceed
3^{d}
, due to a simple covering argument), we conclude that
|K|\geq 3\upsilon|H_{1}|
, thus

(5.80)

\prod_{p\in H_{1}}\mathbbm{1}_{p\sim[s+1:s+n]}\leq\sum_{K:K\subseteq[s+1:s+n],\,|K|\geq 3\upsilon|H_{1}|}\,\prod_{p^{\prime}\in K}\mathbbm{1}_{p^{\prime}\sim H_{1}}.

Denote the integral in (
5.79
) by
(\mathrm{Int})
, then by (
5.80
) we have

(5.81)

\displaystyle\mathrm{(Int)}\leq\sum_{K:K\subseteq[s+1:s+n],\,|K|\geq 3\upsilon|H_{1}|}\int_{\mathcal{D}_{[s+1:s+n]}}\prod_{p^{\prime}\in K}\mathbbm{1}_{p^{\prime}\sim H_{1}}\cdot(f^{\mathcal{A}}(0))^{\otimes[s+1:s+n]}\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{[s+1:s+n]}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{K:K\subseteq[s+1:s+n],\,|K|\geq 3\upsilon|H_{1}|}\int_{\mathcal{D}_{[s+1:s+n]\backslash K}}(f^{\mathcal{A}}(0))^{\otimes[s+1:s+n]\backslash K}\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{[s+1:s+n]\backslash K}\cdot\prod_{p^{\prime}\in K}\int\mathbbm{1}_{p^{\prime}\sim H_{1}}f^{\mathcal{A}}(0,z_{p^{\prime}})\,\mathrm{d}z_{p^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{K:K\subseteq[s+1:s+n],\,|K|\geq 3\upsilon|H_{1}|}\varepsilon^{(d-\frac{1}{2})|K|}\int_{\mathcal{D}_{[s+1:s+n]\backslash K}}(f^{\mathcal{A}}(0))^{\otimes[s+1:s+n]\backslash K}\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{[s+1:s+n]\backslash K}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{3\upsilon|H_{1}|\leq m\leq n}\varepsilon^{(d-\frac{1}{2})m}\binom{n}{m}\int_{\mathcal{D}_{[n-m]}}(f^{\mathcal{A}}(0))^{\otimes[n-m]}\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{[n-m]}.

Here in the third line we have used
\int\mathbbm{1}_{p^{\prime}\sim H_{1}}f^{\mathcal{A}}(0,z_{p^{\prime}})\,\mathrm{d}z_{p^{\prime}}\leq|H_{1}|\varepsilon^{d-1}\leq\varepsilon^{d-\frac{1}{2}}
. In the fourth line we have fixed
|K|=m
which leaves
\binom{n}{m}
choices for
K
, and renamed
\mathcal{D}_{[s+1:s+n]\backslash K}
into
\mathcal{D}_{[n-m]}
.

Finally, inserting (
5.81
) into (
5.79
), we get

(5.82)

\displaystyle|E_{H_{0}}(0,\bm{z}_{H_{0}})|\leq(f^{\mathcal{A}}(0))^{\otimes H_{0}}\frac{1}{\mathcal{Z}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\varepsilon^{-(d-1)n}}{n!}\sum_{(H_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2}):H_{1}\cup p(\mathcal{L}_{2})=H_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{H_{1}\not\sim H_{1}}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{H_{1}\not\sim([s]\backslash H_{1})}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{\mathrm{Pen}}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{3\upsilon|H_{1}|\leq m\leq n}\varepsilon^{(d-\frac{1}{2})m}\binom{n}{m}\int_{\mathcal{D}_{[n-m]}}(f^{\mathcal{A}}(0))^{\otimes[n-m]}\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{[n-m]}

\displaystyle=(f^{\mathcal{A}}(0))^{\otimes H_{0}}\sum_{H_{1}\cup p(\mathcal{L}_{2})=H_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\sum_{m\geq 3\upsilon|H_{1}|}\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{m}{2}}}{m!}\frac{1}{\mathcal{Z}}\underbrace{\left(\sum_{n=m}^{\infty}\frac{\varepsilon^{-(d-1)(n-m)}}{(n-m)!}\int_{\mathcal{D}_{[n-m]}}(f^{\mathcal{A}}(0))^{\otimes[n-m]}\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{[n-m]}\right)}_{=\mathcal{Z},\textrm{ by \eqref{eq.partition_func} and the change of variable }N=n-m}

\displaystyle=(f^{\mathcal{A}}(0))^{\otimes H_{0}}\sum_{H_{1}\cup p(\mathcal{L}_{2})=H_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\sum_{m\geq 3\upsilon|H_{1}|}\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{m}{2}}}{m!}\leq\sum_{H_{1}\cup p(\mathcal{L}_{2})=H_{0}}\varepsilon^{\upsilon|H_{1}|}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(f^{\mathcal{A}}(0))^{\otimes H_{0}}\sum_{\mathcal{L}:p(\mathcal{L})=H_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\varepsilon}.

Here in the last step, we choose
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}_{1}\cup\mathcal{L}_{2}
, where
\mathcal{L}_{1}
is an arbitrary forest with vertex set
H_{1}
; since
\mathbbm{1}_{p_{1}\sim_{\mathrm{in}}p_{2}}\geq\varepsilon^{\upsilon}
, it follows that
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\varepsilon}\geq\varepsilon^{\upsilon|H_{1}|}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}
. This completes the proof.
∎

5.6.
Proof of Proposition
3.25

In this subsection, we put together Propositions
5.16
and
5.18
, to prove Proposition
3.25
.

Proof of Proposition
3.25
.

We divide the proof into 5 parts.

Proof part 1.
We prove there exists
E_{H}=E_{H}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{H})
that verifies (
3.15
). In fact, this follows immediately by induction on
\ell
, using Proposition
5.18
(base case
\ell=0
) and Proposition
5.16
(inductive step).

Proof part 2.
We prove that
f^{\mathcal{A}}
in (
3.15
) satisfies (
3.16
). In fact, we have
f^{\mathcal{A}}(0)=f_{0}
by Proposition
5.18
; by
5.43
), we see that
f^{\mathcal{A}}(\ell\tau)
satisfies

(5.83)

f^{\mathcal{A}}(\ell\tau,z)=\sum_{[\mathbb{M}]\in\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}}\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M},\varnothing}^{\mathrm{Pen}}(z)

with
z=\bm{z}_{r(\mathbb{M})}
. Moreover, by first line of (
5.42
), we see that
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M},\varnothing}^{\mathrm{Pen}}(z)
is given by

(5.84)

\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M},\varnothing}^{\mathrm{Pen}}(z)=\varepsilon^{-(d-1)|\mathbb{M}|_{p\backslash r}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d|\mathbb{M}|_{p\backslash r}}}(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}}^{\mathrm{Pen}}\left[\left(f^{\mathcal{A}}((\ell-1)\tau)\right)^{\otimes(p(\mathbb{M}))}\right]\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{(p\backslash r)(\mathbb{M})}.

Recall also the quantity
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}}(z)
defined in (
3.11
) in Definition
3.23
; using that
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\varepsilon}=1
(
[\mathbb{M}]\in\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}
has no initial link) and
\ell_{1}[{\bm{p}}]-\underline{\ell}=0
(
\mathbb{M}
has only one layer
\ell
), we see that
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}}(z)
is the same as
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M},\varnothing}^{\mathrm{Pen}}(z)
but with
(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}}^{\mathrm{Pen}}
in (
5.84
) replaced by
(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}}
. Similarly, the quantity
|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M},\varnothing}|(z)
defined in (
5.42
) is identical with the quantity
|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}}|(z)
defined in (
3.12
).

Now, by inserting (
5.13
) into (
5.84
), we get

(5.85)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M},\varnothing}^{\mathrm{Pen}}(z)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-(d-1)|\mathbb{M}|_{p\backslash r}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d|\mathbb{M}|_{p\backslash r}}}\bigg[(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}}\left(f^{\mathcal{A}}((\ell-1)\tau)\right)^{\otimes(p(\mathbb{M}))}

\displaystyle+\sum_{(e,e^{\prime})}O_{1}\left(|(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}}||f^{\mathcal{A}}((\ell-1)\tau)|^{\otimes(p(\mathbb{M}))}\right)\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{(p\backslash r)(\mathbb{M})}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}}(z)+\sum_{(e,e^{\prime})}O_{1}\big(|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}}|(z)\big).

Here
|O_{1}(A)|\leq A
, and
\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}=\mathbb{M}_{e,e^{\prime}}
is obtained from
\mathbb{M}
by adding one more overlap
\mathfrak{o}
joining edges
e
and
e^{\prime}
, so that
[\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}]\in\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{err}}
if
[\mathbb{M}]\in\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}
.

By inserting (
5.85
) into (
5.83
), we get

f^{\mathcal{A}}(\ell\tau,z)=\sum_{[\mathbb{M}]\in\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}}\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}}(z)+\sum_{(\mathbb{M},\widetilde{\mathbb{M}})}O_{1}\big(|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}}|(z)\big),

where
[\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}]\in\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{err}}
is obtained from
[\mathbb{M}]\in\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}
by adding one O-atom as above. This completes the proof of (
3.16
), since the number of
[\mathbb{M}]
with fixed
[\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}]
is bounded by
|\log\varepsilon|^{C^{*}}
.

Proof part 3.
We prove that
E_{H}
in (
3.15
) satisfies (
3.17
). In fact, by (
5.44
) and the second line of (
5.42
), we have the recurrence inequality

(5.86)

\displaystyle|E_{H_{\ell}}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{H_{\ell}})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}},\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})=H_{\ell}\\
H_{\ell-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell}),\,r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})\searrow H_{\ell-1}\end{subarray}}\varepsilon^{-(d-1)|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}|_{p\backslash r}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}|_{p\backslash r}}}|(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}|

\displaystyle\left[\left|f^{\mathcal{A}}((\ell-1)\tau)\right|^{\otimes(p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})\backslash H_{\ell-1})}\cdot|E_{H_{\ell-1}}((\ell-1)\tau,\bm{z}_{H_{\ell-1}})|\right]\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{(p\backslash r)(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})}.

This inequality can be schematically written as
|E_{H_{\ell}}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{H_{\ell}})|\leq\mathrm{Op}_{\ell}(|E_{H_{\ell-1}}((\ell-1)\tau,\bm{z}_{H_{\ell-1}})|)
. Iterating it then yields that
|E_{H_{\ell}}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{H_{\ell}})|\leq(\mathrm{Op}_{\ell}\circ\cdots\circ\mathrm{Op}_{1})(|E_{H_{0}}(0,\bm{z}_{H_{0}})|)
. Using also (
5.71
), this leads to

(5.87)

\displaystyle|E_{H_{\ell}}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{H_{\ell}})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}([\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}])_{\ell^{\prime}\in[1:\ell]}:\,[\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell^{\prime}}}\\
r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})=H_{\ell^{\prime}},\,H_{\ell^{\prime}-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})\\
r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})\searrow H_{\ell^{\prime}-1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\mathcal{L}}\prod_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}\varepsilon^{-(d-1)|\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}|_{p\backslash r}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d|\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}|_{p\backslash r}}}\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{(p\backslash r)(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}|(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}}|\bigg(\prod_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}\left|f^{\mathcal{A}}((\ell^{\prime}-1)\tau)\right|^{\otimes(p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})\backslash H_{\ell^{\prime}-1})}|f^{\mathcal{A}}(0)|^{\otimes(H_{0})}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\varepsilon}\bigg),

where in the product
\prod_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}
, the higher values
\ell^{\prime}
occur on the left; also
\mathcal{L}
is as in Proposition
5.18
. Note that (
5.87
) is a sum over equivalence classed of single layer molecules
[\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}]
for each
\ell^{\prime}\in[1:\ell]
, and the summand is constant in each equivalence class (which is easy to verify). Then, from now on, we may treat these equivalence classes as unlabeled molecules (cf. Remark
3.4
).

Constructing
\mathbb{M}
from
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}
.
Next, we shall combine all these
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}
into one multi-layer molecule
\mathbb{M}
by the following procedure: for each
\ell^{\prime}\in[1:\ell-1]
, note that
H_{\ell^{\prime}}=r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})\cap p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}+1})
in (
5.87
). For each particle line in
H_{\ell^{\prime}}
, it has a unique top end in
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}
and a unique bottom end in
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}+1}
, and we merge them into one bond. See
Figure
11
.

Figure 11.
Construction of
\mathbb{M}
from single layer molecules
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}
. Here
\ell=3
; the red (resp. blue and green) edges belong to
H_{3}=r(\mathbb{M}_{3})
(resp.
H_{2}
and
H_{1}
). For example, in the left picture, each of the 3 blue top ends in
\mathbb{M}_{2}
and 3 bottom ends in
\mathbb{M}_{3}
belongs to a particle line in
H_{2}=r(\mathbb{M}_{2})
; in the right picture, we merge the blue ends that belong to the same particle line, to form 3 blue bonds.

Expressing
E_{H_{\ell}}
using
\mathbb{M}
.
With the above correspondence between
\mathbb{M}
and
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}
, we can verify that

(5.88)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}([\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}])_{\ell^{\prime}\in[1:\ell]}:\,[\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell^{\prime}}}\\
r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})=H_{\ell^{\prime}},\,H_{\ell^{\prime}-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})\\
r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})\searrow H_{\ell^{\prime}-1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\mathcal{L}}(\cdots)=\sum_{[\mathbb{M}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\bm{\Lambda}_{\ell^{\prime}}},r(\mathbb{M})=H_{\ell}}(\cdots),

for the summation in (
5.87
), where
\mathcal{F}_{\bm{\Lambda}_{\ell}}
is defined in Definition
3.20
(
1
). Here we note that

•

Properties (
1a
)–(
1c
) in Definition
3.20
exactly correspond to the assumptions
[\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell^{\prime}}}
in (
5.87
), cf. Definition
5.11
and Definition
5.7
(
3
); note that the set
[s]
in Definition
5.7
(
3a
) should be replaced by
H_{\ell}
(which is another fixed set).

•

Property (
1d
) in Definition
3.20
exactly correspond to the assumptions
r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})\searrow H_{\ell^{\prime}-1}
in (
5.87
), cf. Definition
5.14
. Note that for
\ell^{\prime}=1
, the set
H_{0}
is precisely those involved in initial links
\mathcal{L}
(see Proposition
5.18
), which is also consistent with Definition
3.20
(
1d
).

•

Finally, the assumptions
r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})=H_{\ell^{\prime}}
in (
5.87
) uniquely links
H_{\ell^{\prime}}
to
\mathbb{M}
, and the assumptions
H_{\ell^{\prime}-1}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})
allows to define the above merging process to form
\mathbb{M}
.

It is also easy to see that

(5.89)

(p\backslash r)(\mathbb{M})=\bigsqcup_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}(p\backslash r)(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}),\qquad p(\mathbb{M})=\bigsqcup_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}(p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})\backslash H_{\ell^{\prime}-1})\sqcup H_{0},

using (
3.5
)–(
3.6
) in Definition
3.18
. In particular, we may identify the measures and the input factors:

(5.90)

\prod_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}\left(\varepsilon^{-(d-1)|\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}|_{p\backslash r}}\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{(p\backslash r)(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})}\right)=\varepsilon^{-(d-1)|\mathbb{M}|_{p\backslash r}}\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{(p\backslash r)(\mathbb{M})},

(5.91)

\prod_{\ell^{\prime}}\left|f^{\mathcal{A}}((\ell^{\prime}-1)\tau)\right|^{\otimes(p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})\backslash H_{\ell^{\prime}-1})}|f^{\mathcal{A}}(0)|^{\otimes(H_{0})}=\prod_{{\bm{p}}\in p(\mathbb{M})}|f^{\mathcal{A}}((\ell_{1}[{\bm{p}}]-1)\tau)|,

where
\ell_{1}[{\bm{p}}]=\ell^{\prime}
if
{\bm{p}}\in p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})\backslash H_{\ell^{\prime}-1}
and
\ell_{1}[{\bm{p}}]=1
if
{\bm{p}}\in H_{0}
, which follows from (
3.5
)–(
3.6
) and
H_{\ell^{\prime}}=r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})
.

Next we treat the composition operator
\prod_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}|(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}}|
in (
5.87
). We claim that

(5.92)

\prod_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}|(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}}|=\mathcal{R}_{2}^{-1}\circ|(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}}|\circ\mathcal{R}_{1}^{-1},

where
\mathcal{R}_{j}f:=f\circ\mathcal{T}_{j}^{-1}
with

(5.93)

\mathcal{T}_{1}:(x_{\bm{p}}^{\prime},v_{\bm{p}}^{\prime})\mapsto(x_{\bm{p}}^{\prime}+(\ell_{1}[{\bm{p}}]-1)\tau\cdot v_{\bm{p}}^{\prime},v_{\bm{p}}^{\prime}),\quad\mathcal{T}_{2}:(x_{\bm{p}},v_{\bm{p}})\mapsto(x_{\bm{p}}+(\ell-\ell_{2}[{\bm{p}}])_{+}\tau\cdot v_{\bm{p}},v_{\bm{p}}).

To prove (
5.92
), recall the flow map
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{M}}
defined in Definition
3.22
(
1
). Using that
|(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}}|f=(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{M}}\cdot f)\circ\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{M}}^{-1}
(see (
3.10
), note that
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{M}}
is injective on the support of
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{M}}
) and similarly for
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}
, we only need to prove

(5.94)

\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{M}}(\ell\tau)=\mathcal{T}_{2}\circ(\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}(\tau)\circ\cdots\circ\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{M}_{1}}(\tau))\circ\mathcal{T}_{1}

on the support of
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{M}}
. To prove (
5.94
), simply note that in the prescribed dynamics
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{M}}
, each particle
{\bm{p}}
first moves linearly from time
0
to time
(\ell_{1}[{\bm{p}}]-1)\tau
(i.e. before its start layer
\ell_{1}[{\bm{p}}]
at which time
{\bm{p}}
starts to occur in the
\mathbb{M}
-prescribed dynamics), then moves according to the dynamics
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}(\tau)\circ\cdots\circ\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{M}_{1}}(\tau)
(with only those in the lifespan of
{\bm{p}}
having effect), and then moves linearly from time
9
9

9

The
\ell_{2}[{\bm{p}}]
may be
\ell+1
by convention, but the relevant time would still be
\ell\tau
, hence the
(\ell-\ell_{2}[{\bm{p}}])_{+}
in (
5.93
).

\ell_{2}[{\bm{p}}]\tau
to time
\ell\tau
(i.e. after the finish layer
\ell_{2}[{\bm{p}}]
at which time
{\bm{p}}
no longer occurs in the
\mathbb{M}
-prescribed dynamics). By composing these three parts of dynamics, we obtain (
5.94
) and hence (
5.92
).

Now, using (
5.92
), we can replace the
\prod_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}|(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}}|
in (
5.87
), up to the two linear transports
\mathcal{R}_{1}^{-1}
and
\mathcal{R}_{2}
. The
\mathcal{R}_{1}^{-1}
acts on the
f^{\mathcal{A}}
factors and turns them into
|f^{\mathcal{A}}((\ell_{1}[{\bm{p}}]-1)\tau,x_{\bm{p}}^{\prime}+(\ell_{1}[{\bm{p}}]-1)\tau\cdot v_{\bm{p}}^{\prime},v_{\bm{p}}^{\prime})|
as occur in (
3.13
) with
\underline{\ell}=1
, and does not affect
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\varepsilon}
as
\ell_{1}[{\bm{p}}]=1
for initial links. The
\mathcal{R}_{2}^{-1}
has not effect on the integral, as they act only on the non-root variables of
\mathbb{M}
(for root particles
{\bm{p}}\in r(\mathbb{M})
we have
r_{2}[{\bm{p}}]\in\{\ell,\ell+1\}
thus
(\ell-\ell_{2}[{\bm{p}}])_{+}=0
) which are integrated, and the linear transport
\mathcal{R}_{2}^{-1}
preserves integration.

Finishing the proof of (
3.17
).
Let
H=H_{\ell}
. By putting together all the above and inserting (
5.88
), (
5.90
)–(
5.92
) into (
5.87
), we get

(5.95)

|E_{H}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{H})|\leq\sum_{[\mathbb{M}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\bm{\Lambda}_{\ell}},r(\mathbb{M})=H}\varepsilon^{-(d-1)|\mathbb{M}|_{p\backslash r}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d|\mathbb{M}|_{p\backslash r}}}|(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}}|\\
\bigg(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\prod_{{\bm{p}}\in p(\mathbb{M})}|f^{\mathcal{A}}((\ell_{1}[{\bm{p}}]-1)\tau,x_{\bm{p}}^{\prime}+(\ell_{1}[{\bm{p}}]-\underline{\ell})\tau\cdot v_{\bm{p}}^{\prime},v_{\bm{p}}^{\prime})|\bigg)\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{(p\backslash r)(\mathbb{M})},

which proves (
3.17
).

Proof part 4.
We prove that
\mathrm{Err}
in (
3.15
) satisfies (
3.18
)–(
3.19
). Note
\mathrm{Err}(0)=0
by (
5.70
). We define
\mathrm{Err}^{1}
and
\mathrm{Err}^{2}
as in Proposition
5.16
(
3
), then (
3.19
) follows immediately from (
5.46
). The estimate (
3.18
) for
\mathrm{Err}^{1}
follows from the same argument as in the proof of (
3.17
) in Part 3 above (starting from (
5.45
)), with the only difference being the layer
\ell
molecule
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}
: we have
\mathbb{M}_{\ell}\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{err}}
instead of
\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}
, that
p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})=Q_{\ell}
instead of
H_{\ell}
, and do not require
r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})\searrow H_{\ell-1}
. This difference leads to the condition for
\mathcal{F}_{\bm{\Lambda}_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{err}}
in Definition
3.20
(
2
), hence
\mathbb{M}\in\mathcal{F}_{\bm{\Lambda}_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{err}}
in (
3.18
). This completes the proof of (
3.18
)–(
3.19
).

Proof part 5.
Finally we prove that
f_{s}^{\mathrm{err}}
in (
3.14
) satisfies (
3.20
). Recall that this
f_{s}^{\mathrm{err}}
is defined in (
4.18
). We start with an upper bound for
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\Lambda,\Gamma}}
in Definition
4.6
.

Upper bound for
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\Lambda,\Gamma}}
.
We first prove the following upper bound:

(5.96)

\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\Lambda,\Gamma}}\leq\sum_{\mathbb{M}\in\mathscr{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{trc.err}},\,r(\mathbb{M})=[s]}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{M}},

where
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{M}}
is defined in (
3.8
) in Definition
3.22
.
\mathscr{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{trc.err}}
is the collection of all labeled molecules
\mathbb{M}
of a single layer
\ell
that is connected apart from possible empty ends (which are root particle lines), has only C-atoms, and satisfies (
3.7
) in Definition
3.20
(
4
). Define
\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{trc.err}}
to be the set of corresponding equivalence classes.

In fact, to prove (
5.96
), we only need to prove that, if
\bm{z}^{0}\in\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\Lambda,\Gamma}
, then there exists
\mathbb{M}\in\mathscr{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{trc.err}}
such that
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{M}}(\bm{z}^{0})=1
. To prove this, consider the trajectory
\bm{z}^{E}(t)
defined by the E-dynamics with initial configuration
\bm{z}^{0}
. For each time
t\in[0,\tau]
, define
\mathbb{M}(t)
to be the C-molecule topological reduction of the trajectory up to time
t
.

By Definition
4.6
, we know that for
t=\tau
, the molecule
\mathbb{M}(\tau)
has a component
\mathbb{M}_{i}
with either
|\mathbb{M}_{i}|_{p}>\Lambda
or
\rho(\mathbb{M}_{i})>\Gamma
; in this case we say
\mathbb{M}_{i}
is
large
. Consider the
first time

t_{*}
such that
\mathbb{M}(t_{*}^{+})
has a large component
\mathbb{M}_{i}
; there must be a collision
\mathfrak{n}
happening at
t_{*}
, such that
\mathbb{M}=\mathbb{M}(t_{*}^{+})
is obtained from
\mathbb{M}(t_{*}^{-})
by adding one more collision
\mathfrak{n}
. Note that this collision either happens within a component
\mathbb{M}_{j}
of
\mathbb{M}(t_{*}^{-})
(so does not affect
|\mathbb{M}_{j}|_{p}
and increases
\rho(\mathbb{M}_{j})
by
1
), or merges two components
\mathbb{M}_{j}
and
\mathbb{M}_{k}
of
\mathbb{M}(t_{*}^{-})
together (so the new component
\mathbb{M}_{i}
of
\mathbb{M}(t_{*}^{+})
satisfies
|\mathbb{M}_{i}|_{p}=|\mathbb{M}_{j}|_{p}+|\mathbb{M}_{k}|_{p}
and
\rho(\mathbb{M}_{i})=\rho(\mathbb{M}_{j})+\rho(\mathbb{M}_{k})
). By definition of
t_{*}
, we know that no component of
\mathbb{M}(t_{*}^{-})
is large, so
|\mathbb{M}_{j}|_{p}\leq\Lambda
and
\rho(\mathbb{M}_{j})\leq\Gamma
, and the same for
\mathbb{M}_{k}
. In particular, we know that
\mathbb{M}_{i}
is large, and
|\mathbb{M}_{i}|_{p}\leq 2\Lambda_{\ell}
and
\rho(\mathbb{M}_{i})\leq 2\Gamma
.

Now, let
\mathbb{M}
be the molecule formed by adding to
\mathbb{M}_{i}
the empty ends corresponding to each root particle in
[s]
that does not belong to
\mathbb{M}_{i}
(these particles move linearly without any collision), with
p(\mathbb{M})=[s]\cup p(\mathbb{M}_{i})
and
r(\mathbb{M})=[s]
. By the above property of
\mathbb{M}_{i}
, and using that
s\leq\Lambda_{\ell}
, it is easy to see that
\mathbb{M}
satisfies (
3.7
). Moreover the fact that
\mathbb{M}_{i}
is a component of the C-molecule topological reduction
\mathbb{M}
of the E-dynamics, implies that
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{M}_{i}}=1
(see the proof of Lemma
5.9
), and thus
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{M}}=1
. This proves (
5.96
).

Finishing the proof of (
3.20
).
Recall from (
4.16
) and (
4.18
) that

(5.97)

f_{s}^{\mathrm{err}}(t_{\mathrm{fin}},\bm{z}_{s})=\varepsilon^{s(d-1)}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}W_{s+n}^{\mathrm{err}}(t_{\mathrm{fin}},\bm{z}_{s+n})=\varepsilon^{s(d-1)}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\mathfrak{L}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\big[(\mathcal{S}^{E}_{s+n}(\tau))^{{\mathfrak{L}}-\ell}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\ell,s+n}^{\mathrm{err}}(\tau)\big](\widetilde{W}_{s+n}((\ell-1)\tau)).

Now, using (
5.96
), the definition
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\ell}=\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\Lambda_{\ell},\Gamma}
, the definition of
\mathcal{S}_{\ell,N}^{\mathrm{err}}
in (
4.12
), and the
L^{1}
preserving property of
\mathcal{S}_{N}^{E}
and
\mathcal{S}_{N}^{\ell}
(Proposition
4.5
(
4
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\|f_{s}^{\mathrm{err}}(t_{\mathrm{fin}},\bm{z}_{s})\|_{L_{\bm{z}_{s}}^{1}}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{s(d-1)}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\mathfrak{L}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\big\|\big[(\mathcal{S}^{E}_{s+n}(\tau))^{{\mathfrak{L}}-\ell}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\ell,s+n}^{\mathrm{err}}(\tau)\big](\widetilde{W}_{s+n}((\ell-1)\tau))\big\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon^{s(d-1)}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\mathfrak{L}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\big\|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{s+n}^{\ell}}\cdot\widetilde{W}_{s+n}((\ell-1)\tau)\big\|_{L^{1}}

(5.98)

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon^{s(d-1)}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\mathfrak{L}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}\in\mathscr{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{trc.err}},\,r({\mathbb{M}_{\ell}})=[s]}\frac{1}{n!}\big\|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}\cdot\widetilde{W}_{s+n}((\ell-1)\tau)\big\|_{L^{1}}.

In the sum (
5.98
), we argue as in the proof of Proposition
5.12
: by symmetry we may always restrict
p({\mathbb{M}_{\ell}})=[|{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}|_{p}]
with an additional factor
\binom{n}{|{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}|_{p}-s}
. Using also (
5.34
) which is also true in the current setting, we obtain that

\displaystyle\|f_{s}^{\mathrm{err}}(t_{\mathrm{fin}},\bm{z}_{s})\|_{L_{\bm{z}_{s}}^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon^{s(d-1)}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\mathfrak{L}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}\in\mathscr{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{trc.err}},\,r({\mathbb{M}_{\ell}})=[s]}\frac{1}{n!}\big\|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}\cdot\widetilde{W}_{s+n}((\ell-1)\tau)\big\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon^{s(d-1)}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\mathfrak{L}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}\in\mathscr{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{trc.err}}\\
p({\mathbb{M}_{\ell}})=[|{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}|_{p}],\,r({\mathbb{M}_{\ell}})=[s]\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{n!}\binom{n}{|{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}|_{p}-s}\big\|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}\cdot\widetilde{W}_{s+n}((\ell-1)\tau)\big\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle=2\varepsilon^{s(d-1)}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\mathfrak{L}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{[{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{trc.err}},\,r({\mathbb{M}_{\ell}})=[s]}\frac{1}{(n-|{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}|_{p}+s)!}\big\|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}\cdot\widetilde{W}_{s+n}((\ell-1)\tau)\big\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle=2\varepsilon^{s(d-1)}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\mathfrak{L}}\sum_{[{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{trc.err}},\,r({\mathbb{M}_{\ell}})=[s]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d|{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}|_{p}}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}(\bm{z}_{p({\mathbb{M}_{\ell}})})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{p({\mathbb{M}_{\ell}})}

(5.99)

\displaystyle\phantom{2\varepsilon^{s(d-1)}}\times\sum_{n=|{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}|_{p}-s}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(n-|{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}|_{p}+s)!}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d(n-|{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}|_{p}+s)}}\widetilde{W}_{s+n}((\ell-1)\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{[s+n]\backslash p({\mathbb{M}_{\ell}})}.

Since the summation in (
5.99
) is equal to
\varepsilon^{-(d-2)|{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}|_{p}}\widetilde{f}_{|{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}|_{p}}((\ell-1)\tau,\bm{z}_{p({\mathbb{M}_{\ell}})})
by (
4.17
), and using also the
L^{1}
preserving property of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}
, we conclude that

(5.100)

\displaystyle\|f_{s}^{\mathrm{err}}(t_{\mathrm{fin}},\bm{z}_{s})\|_{L_{\bm{z}_{s}}^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{\ell=1}^{\mathfrak{L}}\sum_{[{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{trc.err}},\,r({\mathbb{M}_{\ell}})=[s]}\varepsilon^{-(d-1)|{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}|_{p\backslash r}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d|{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}|_{p}}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}\cdot\widetilde{f}_{|{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}|_{p}}((\ell-1)\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{p({\mathbb{M}_{\ell}})}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{\ell=1}^{\mathfrak{L}}\sum_{[{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{trc.err}},\,r({\mathbb{M}_{\ell}})=[s]}\varepsilon^{-(d-1)|{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}|_{p\backslash r}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d|{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}|_{p}}}|(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}||\widetilde{f}_{|{\mathbb{M}_{\ell}}|_{p}}((\ell-1)\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{p({\mathbb{M}_{\ell}})}.

The
\widetilde{f}((\ell-1)\tau)
term in (
5.100
) contains a main term and an error term
\mathrm{Err}
as in (
3.15
). The contribution of
\mathrm{Err}
leads to the second term on the right hand side of (
3.20
), using
|\mathbb{M}_{\ell}|_{p\backslash r}\leq 3\Lambda_{\ell}
and following the proof of (
5.46
) in Part 1 of Proposition
5.16
. For the contribution of the main term in (
3.15
), we repeat the same arguments as in the proof of (
3.17
) and (
3.18
) in Parts 3 and 4 above (i.e. expand
\widetilde{f}((\ell-1)\tau)
into previous layers and putting all different layers into a multi-layer molecule
\mathbb{M}
; note we have extra integration in particles
r(\mathbb{M})=[s]
here). This then leads to the first term on the right hand side of (
3.20
), where
[\mathbb{M}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\bm{\Lambda}_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{trc.err}}
because
[\mathbb{M}_{\ell}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{trc.err}}
, see Definition
3.20
(
4
). This proves (
3.20
), and finally finishes the proof of Proposition
3.25
.
∎

Remark 5.19
.

With Proposition
3.25
proved, in the rest of this paper, we need to estimate the quantities
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}}
and
|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}}|
on the right hand side of (
3.16
)–(
3.20
). Note that these quantities are constant in equivalence classes
[\mathbb{M}]
. Thus, as said in Remark
3.4
, for the rest of this paper, we will use
\mathbb{M}
to denote unlabeled molecules only (plus labelings of root particles only).

6.
Statements of cumulant and
f^{\mathcal{A}}
estimates

In this section we will use Proposition
3.25
to reduce Theorem
1
to the 3 main estimates (see Propositions Propositions
6.1
–
6.3
below), about the quantities
f^{\mathcal{A}}
,
E_{H}
,
\mathrm{Err}^{1}
,
\mathrm{Err}^{2}
and
f^{\mathrm{err}}
defined in Proposition
3.25
. These propositions will be the main goal in the rest of the paper, whose proof will occupy Sections
7
–
15
.

In the first proposition, we show that
f^{\mathcal{A}}
is indeed an approximate solution to the Boltzmann equation.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
f^{\mathcal{A}}
be defined as in Proposition
3.25
(
1
), inductively via (
3.16
), with
f^{\mathcal{A}}(0,z)=f_{0}(z)
; also recall
\beta_{\ell}
and
\theta_{\ell}
defined in (
1.34
) and
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{Bol}^{\beta_{\ell}}}
defined by (
1.15
). Then we have

(6.1)

f^{\mathcal{A}}(\ell\tau,z)=f(\ell\tau,z)+(f^{\mathcal{A}})_{\mathrm{rem}}(\ell\tau,z),

where
f(t,z)
is the solution to the Boltzmann equation as in Theorem
1
, and
(f^{\mathcal{A}})_{\mathrm{rem}}
satisfies that

(6.2)

\|(f^{\mathcal{A}})_{\mathrm{rem}}(\ell\tau)\|_{\mathrm{Bol}^{\beta_{\ell}}}\leq\varepsilon^{\theta_{\ell}}.

Proof.

The proof will occupy Section
14
, and will be finished in Section
14.3
.
∎

In the second proposition, we prove upper bounds for
E_{H}
and
\mathrm{Err}_{\ell}^{1}
. This is the crucial proposition, and will be the main goal of the second part of the paper.

Proposition 6.2
.

Recall the
E_{H}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{H})
and
\mathrm{Err}^{1}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{s})
introduced in Proposition
3.25
, and also the notion
C_{j}^{*}
in (
1.31
) in Definition
1.6
(
2
). Assume that the estimates (
6.1
)–(
6.2
) hold with
\ell
replaced by any
\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell-1
. Then we have

(6.3)

\|E_{H}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{H})\|_{L^{1}}\leq\varepsilon^{3^{-d-2}+(C_{14}^{*})^{-1}\cdot|H|}

for any
H\neq\varnothing
, and

(6.4)

\|\mathrm{Err}^{1}(\ell\tau,\bm{z}_{s})\|_{L^{1}}\leq\tau^{\Lambda_{\ell}/10}.

Proof.

The proof will occupy Sections
7
–
9
, and will be finished in Section
9.2
, pending a crucial combinatorial statement, Proposition
9.7
. Then, the proof of Proposition
9.7
will be the main goal of the third part of the paper, and will occupy Sections
10
–
13
.
∎

In the last proposition we prove upper bounds for
f_{s}^{\mathrm{err}}(t_{\mathrm{fin}})
.

Proposition 6.3
.

Recall the
f_{s}^{\mathrm{err}}(t_{\mathrm{fin}},\bm{z}_{s})
introduced in Proposition
3.25
. Then we have

(6.5)

\|f_{s}^{\mathrm{err}}(t_{\mathrm{fin}},\bm{z}_{s})\|_{L^{1}}\leq\varepsilon^{3^{-d-2}}.

Proof.

The proof will occupy Section
15
, based on Proposition
15.1
which will be proved in Section
15.1
.
∎

Now we prove Theorem
1
assuming Propositions
6.1
–
6.3
.

Proof of Theorem
1
assuming Propositions
6.1
–
6.3
.

The goal is to prove (
1.18
) for
f_{s}(t_{\mathrm{fin}},\bm{z}_{s})
(recall we have set
t=t_{\mathrm{fin}}=\mathfrak{L}\tau
), with
s\leq|\log\varepsilon|=A_{\mathfrak{L}}
. By (
3.14
) and (
6.5
), we may replace
f_{s}(t_{\mathrm{fin}},\bm{z}_{s})
in (
1.18
) by
\widetilde{f}_{s}(t_{\mathrm{fin}},\bm{z}_{s})
in (
3.15
), which is

(6.6)

\widetilde{f}_{s}(t_{\mathrm{fin}},\bm{z}_{s})=\sum_{H\subseteq[s]}(f^{\mathcal{A}}(t_{\mathrm{fin}}))^{\otimes([s]\backslash H)}\cdot E_{H}(t_{\mathrm{fin}},\bm{z}_{H})+\mathrm{Err}(\mathfrak{L}\tau,\bm{z}_{s}).

By iteratively applying the
\mathrm{Err}^{2}
estimate (
3.19
) in Proposition
3.25
and combining with (
6.4
), we get that

\|\mathrm{Err}(\mathfrak{L}\tau,\bm{z}_{s})\|_{L^{1}}\leq\sum_{\ell=0}^{\mathfrak{L}}\tau^{\Lambda_{\ell}/10}\cdot\prod_{\ell^{\prime}=\ell+1}^{\mathfrak{L}}\varepsilon^{-(d-1)\Lambda_{\ell^{\prime}}^{2}A_{\ell^{\prime}}}

which is negligible thanks to our choice of the sequences
(\Lambda_{\ell})
and
(A_{\ell})
in (
1.33
) in Definition
1.6
(
3
), which ensures that
\Lambda_{\ell}\gg\Lambda_{\ell^{\prime}}^{2}A_{\ell^{\prime}}
if
\ell^{\prime}>\ell
and
\tau^{\Lambda_{\ell}/10}\cdot\varepsilon^{-(d-1)\Lambda_{\ell^{\prime}}^{2}A_{\ell^{\prime}}}\ll 1
. Therefore, we may omit the
\mathrm{Err}
term from (
6.6
).

Next, (
6.1
) and (
6.2
) implies that
\|f^{\mathcal{A}}(t_{\mathrm{fin}})-f(t_{\mathrm{fin}})\|_{L^{1}}\leq\varepsilon^{(\theta+\theta_{\mathfrak{L}})/2}
(with
\theta=\theta_{0}/2
and
\theta_{\mathfrak{L}}
defined in Definition
1.6
(
3
)), and thus
\|f^{\mathcal{A}}(t_{\mathrm{fin}})\|_{L^{1}}\leq 1+\varepsilon^{\theta}
using
\int f_{0}(z)\,\mathrm{d}z=1
and the conservation of
L^{1}
norm for the Boltzmann equation (
1.13
). Using also the bound (
6.3
) for
E_{H}
and the fact that
s\leq|\log\varepsilon|
, it follows that the terms corresponding to
H\neq\varnothing
in (
6.6
) are also negligible in
L^{1}
. We then know that the function
\widetilde{f}_{s}(t_{\mathrm{fin}})
is close to the following tensor product up to error
O_{L^{1}}(\varepsilon^{\theta})
:

(f^{\mathcal{A}}(t_{\mathrm{fin}}))^{\otimes[s]}=\prod_{p\in[s]}f^{\mathcal{A}}(t_{\mathrm{fin}},z_{p}).

Once again, by
\|f^{\mathcal{A}}(t_{\mathrm{fin}})-f(t_{\mathrm{fin}})\|_{L^{1}}\leq\varepsilon^{(\theta+\theta_{\mathfrak{L}})/2}
, we know the function
\widetilde{f}_{s}(t_{\mathrm{fin}})
is close to
(f(t_{\mathrm{fin}}))^{\otimes[s]}
up to error
O_{L^{1}}(\varepsilon^{\theta})
. Then (
1.18
) follows by using the following estimates for the indicator function
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{s}}(\bm{z}_{s})

\bigg\|\prod_{p\in[s]}f(t_{\mathrm{fin}},z_{p})\cdot\big(1-\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{s}}(\bm{z}_{s})\big)\bigg\|_{L^{1}}\leq Cs^{2}\varepsilon^{d}\leq\varepsilon^{d/2}

because
s\leq|\log\varepsilon|
. This proves Theorem
1
.
∎

7.
Preparations for
E_{H}
estimates

From this section until Section
13
, we will prove Proposition
6.2
(and the associated Proposition
9.7
). We start from the upper bounds of
E_{H}
and
\mathrm{Err}^{1}
in (
3.17
)–(
3.18
) in Proposition
3.25
, which have the form
\sum_{\mathbb{M}}|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}}|
(here and below we will replace
[\mathbb{M}]
by
\mathbb{M}
as
\mathbb{M}
now represents unlabeled molecules, see Remark
5.19
). To bound these terms, it suffices to (i) control the number of terms
\mathbb{M}
in the summation, and (ii) control the size of
|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}}|
for each individual
\mathbb{M}
.

In this section, first in Section
7.1
we prove an upper bound (Proposition
7.2
) on the number of choices of
\mathbb{M}
, and then in Section
7.2
we reduce the
L^{1}
norm of
|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}}|
to an integral
\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{M}}(Q_{\mathbb{M}})
involving Dirac
\bm{\delta}
functions that localizes each individual collision and overlap (Proposition
7.5
). The estimates of this quantity
\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{M}}(Q_{\mathbb{M}})
will then occupy Sections
8
–
9
.

7.1.
Upper bounds of combinatorial factors

In this subsection we prove an upper bound on the number of molecules
\mathbb{M}
in Proposition
7.2
. For this we need to introduce the following parameter
\rho
for molecules
\mathbb{M}
:

Definition 7.1

(The parameter
\rho
)
.

Given a molecule
\mathbb{M}
with highest layer
\ell
and lowest layer
1
, with layer
\ell^{\prime}
denoted by
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}
. Define the parameters
s_{\ell^{\prime}}
and
\mathfrak{R}_{\ell^{\prime}}
by
s_{\ell^{\prime}}\coloneqq|r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})|
and
\mathfrak{R}_{\ell^{\prime}}\coloneqq\rho(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})
for
\ell^{\prime}\in[1:\ell]
; also define
s_{0}=|H_{0}|
where
H_{0}
is the initial cumulant set (see (
5.87
)), i.e. those particles involved in initial links. Now define the parameter
\rho
by

(7.1)

\rho=\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=0}^{\ell}s_{\ell^{\prime}}+\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}\mathfrak{R}_{\ell^{\prime}}.

We interpret the quantity
\rho
as the sum of
\mathfrak{R}_{\ell^{\prime}}
(the total number of recollisions within each single layer), together with the sum of
s_{\ell^{\prime}}
(the total number of particles lines crossing different layers).

We now state and prove the main combinatorial upper bound in this section.

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
\mathbb{M}
be a molecule and
\rho
be as in Definition
7.1
. Assume
H
is a set and
|H|\leq|\log\varepsilon|^{C^{*}}
. Consider all molecules
\mathbb{M}\in\mathcal{F}_{\bm{\Lambda}_{\ell}}
(recall that we are summing over non-labeled molecules
\mathbb{M}
) that satisfy
r(\mathbb{M})=H
, with the size
|\mathbb{M}|
of
\mathbb{M}
fixed, and the
\rho
defined in Definition
7.1
also fixed. Then the number of choices for
\mathbb{M}
, together with the labelings of root particles, is bounded by

(7.2)

\sum_{\mathbb{M}}1\leq C^{|\mathbb{M}|}\cdot|\log\varepsilon|^{C^{*}\rho}.

The same result is true if we replace
\mathcal{F}_{\bm{\Lambda}_{\ell}}
by
\mathcal{F}_{\bm{\Lambda}_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{err}}
or
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}
or
\mathcal{F}_{\bm{\Lambda}_{\ell}}^{\mathrm{trc.err}}
.

Proof.

We only consider the case
\mathbb{M}\in\mathcal{F}_{\bm{\Lambda}_{\ell}}
, as the other cases are similar. Note that
\mathbb{M}
is constructed from its layers
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}
by the following process, see Part 3 of the proof of Proposition
3.25
and
Figure
11
:

•

Label the root particles, and draw a molecule
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}
with a single layer
\ell^{\prime}
, for each
\ell^{\prime}\in[1:\ell]
;

•

For each
\ell^{\prime}\in[1:\ell-1]
, identify a subset of
p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})
with a subset of
p(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}+1})
, call this identified subset
H_{\ell^{\prime}}
and
|H_{\ell^{\prime}}|=s_{\ell^{\prime}}
;

•

For each particle line in
H_{\ell^{\prime}}
, merge the corresponding top end in
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}
and bottom end in
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}+1}
to form a bond;

•

Finally choose a set
H_{0}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M}_{1})
, and fix the initial links
\mathcal{L}
between particle lines in
H_{0}
as in Proposition
5.18
.

We now estimate the number of choices for
\mathbb{M}
, by considering each of the above steps.

Proof part 1
. In this part we prove that
|\mathbb{M}|\leq|\log\varepsilon|^{C^{*}}
and
|\mathbb{M}|_{p}\leq|\log\varepsilon|^{C^{*}}
. In fact, by Definition
3.20
(
1a
)–(
1b
) we know that each cluster of
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}
has at most
\Lambda_{\ell^{\prime}}
particle lines and at most
\Lambda_{\ell^{\prime}}+\Gamma
collisions (using the upper bound of
\Gamma
for the recollision number). Then each component has at most
\Lambda_{\ell^{\prime}}^{2}
particle lines and at most
2\Lambda_{\ell^{\prime}}^{2}
collisions (as each component has at most
\Lambda_{\ell}
clusters). Then each
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}
has at most
A_{\ell^{\prime}}\Lambda_{\ell^{\prime}}^{2}
particle lines and
2A_{\ell^{\prime}}\Lambda_{\ell^{\prime}}^{2}
collisions (as each component must contain a root particle line, and there are
s_{\ell^{\prime}}\leq A_{\ell^{\prime}}
of them). All these values are bounded as
\leq|\log\varepsilon|^{C^{*}}
by Definition
1.6
(
3
), hence the result.

Proof part 2.
In this part we consider the first step of labeling and constructing individual
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}
. Note that labelings lead to
|H|!\leq|\log\varepsilon|^{C^{*}\rho}
choices as
|H|=|r(\mathbb{M})|=|r(\mathbb{M}_{\ell})|=s_{\ell}\leq\rho
. Next we may fix the values of
(|\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}|)_{\ell^{\prime}\in[1:\ell]}
. As these numbers add up to the fixed
|\mathbb{M}|
, we have at most
2^{|\mathbb{M}|}
choices which is acceptable in (
7.2
). Similarly, we can also fix the values of
(s_{\ell^{\prime}})
and
(\rho(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}))
at an acceptable price. Now, note that each
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}
is a graph with each atom having at most
4
bonds; moreover it is formed by adding at most
\rho(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})
edges to a forest.

It is known that the number of trees (and forests) with
|\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}|
atoms and each atom having at most
4
bonds can be bounded by
10^{|\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}|}
(the result for forests follows from that for trees; for trees, we may fix a root and turn the tree into a rooted, at most ternary tree, and use the well-known counting upper bound for the number of binary and ternary trees).

Once the tree parts are fixed, we add in the
\leq\rho
extra edges one by one; each edge has at most
|\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}|^{2}\leq|\log\varepsilon|^{C^{*}}
choices, which leads to
|\log\varepsilon|^{C^{*}\rho(\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}})}
choices in total. All these factors are acceptable in (
7.2
) even after accounting for different
\ell^{\prime}
(for example
\prod_{\ell^{\prime}}10^{|\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}|}=10^{|\mathbb{M}|}
).

Proof part 3.
In this part we consider the steps of matching and merging particle lines/ends in
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}
and
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}+1}
, see
Figure
11
. Recall that
|H_{\ell^{\prime}}|=s_{\ell^{\prime}}
; selecting a set of this cardinality in
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}
and
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}+1}
leads to at most

\binom{|\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}|_{p}}{s_{\ell^{\prime}}}\binom{|\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}+1}|_{p}}{s_{\ell^{\prime}}}\leq|\log\varepsilon|^{C^{*}s_{\ell^{\prime}}}

choices. Putting different
\ell^{\prime}
together, we again get a factor of
|\log\varepsilon|^{C^{*}\rho}
which is acceptable.

Proof part 4.
In this part we consider the last step of constructing
H_{0}
and
\mathcal{L}
. Note that
|H_{0}|=s_{0}
, and
|\mathcal{L}|
(i.e. the number of initial links in
\mathcal{L}
) is bounded by
2|H_{0}|
as
\mathcal{L}
is a forest. The number of choices of
H_{0}
and
\mathcal{L}
is then trivially bounded by
|\log\varepsilon|^{C^{*}s_{0}}
, which is also acceptable.

By putting Parts 1–4 above together that accounts for all steps in the construction of
\mathbb{M}
, we have proved (
7.2
) and Proposition
7.2
.
∎

7.2.
Reducing to the integral
\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{M}}(Q_{\mathbb{M}})

In this subsection, we rewrite the integrals
|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}}|
in Proposition
3.25
as integrals
\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{M}}(Q_{\mathbb{M}})
involving Dirac functions that localize each individual collision and overlap, see Definition
7.3
and Proposition
7.5
. As we will see in Section
8
, this representation behaves well under the cutting operation, and allows us to obtain efficient estimates.

Definition 7.3

(Associated operator of a molecule)
.

Consider the molecule
\mathbb{M}=(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{L})
, we define the following objects.

(1)

Associated variables
\bm{z}_{\mathcal{E}}
and
\bm{t}_{\mathbb{M}}
.
We associate each atom
\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{M}
with a time variable
t_{\mathfrak{n}}
and each edge
e\in\mathcal{E}
with a position-velocity vector
z_{e}=(x_{e},v_{e})
. We refer to them as
associated variables
and denote the collections of these variables by
\bm{z}_{\mathcal{E}}=(z_{e}:e\in\mathcal{E})
and
\bm{t}_{\mathbb{M}}=(t_{\mathfrak{n}}:\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{M})
.

(2)

Associated distributions
\bm{\Delta}_{\mathfrak{n}}
.
Given an atom
\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{M}
, let
(e_{1},e_{2})
and
(e_{1}^{\prime},e_{2}^{\prime})
be the two bottom edges and top edges at
\mathfrak{n}
respectively, such that
e_{1}
and
e_{1}^{\prime}
(resp.
e_{2}
and
e_{2}^{\prime}
) are serial. We define the
associated distribution

\bm{\Delta}_{\mathfrak{n}}=\bm{\Delta}=\bm{\Delta}(z_{e_{1}},z_{e_{2}},z_{e_{1}^{\prime}},z_{e_{2}^{\prime}},t_{\mathfrak{n}})
as follows.

(a)

If
\mathfrak{n}
is an C-atom, then

(7.3)

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}(z_{e_{1}},z_{e_{2}},z_{e_{1}^{\prime}},z_{e_{2}^{\prime}},t_{\mathfrak{n}})

\displaystyle:=\bm{\delta}\big(x_{e_{1}^{\prime}}-x_{e_{1}}+t_{\mathfrak{n}}(v_{e_{1}^{\prime}}-v_{e_{1}})\big)\cdot\bm{\delta}\big(x_{e_{2}^{\prime}}-x_{e_{2}}+t_{\mathfrak{n}}(v_{e_{2}^{\prime}}-v_{e_{2}})\big)

\displaystyle\times\bm{\delta}\big(|x_{e_{1}}-x_{e_{2}}+t_{\mathfrak{n}}(v_{e_{1}}-v_{e_{2}})|-\varepsilon\big)\cdot\big[(v_{e_{1}}-v_{e_{2}})\cdot\omega\big]_{-}

\displaystyle\times\bm{\delta}\big(v_{e_{1}^{\prime}}-v_{e_{1}}+[(v_{e_{1}}-v_{e_{2}})\cdot\omega]\omega\big)\cdot\bm{\delta}\big(v_{e_{2}^{\prime}}-v_{e_{2}}-[(v_{e_{1}}-v_{e_{2}})\cdot\omega]\omega\big),

where
\omega:=\varepsilon^{-1}(x_{e_{1}}-x_{e_{2}}+t_{\mathfrak{n}}(v_{e_{1}}-v_{e_{2}}))
is a unit vector, and
z_{-}=-\min(z,0)
.

(b)

If
\mathfrak{n}
is an O-atom, then

(7.4)

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}(z_{e_{1}},z_{e_{2}},z_{e_{1}^{\prime}},z_{e_{2}^{\prime}},t_{\mathfrak{n}})

\displaystyle:=\bm{\delta}(x_{e_{1}^{\prime}}-x_{e_{1}})\cdot\bm{\delta}(x_{e_{2}^{\prime}}-x_{e_{2}})

\displaystyle\times\bm{\delta}\big(|x_{e_{1}}-x_{e_{2}}+t_{\mathfrak{n}}(v_{e_{1}}-v_{e_{2}})|-\varepsilon\big)\cdot\big[(v_{e_{1}}-v_{e_{2}})\cdot\omega\big]_{-}

\displaystyle\times\bm{\delta}\big(v_{e_{1}^{\prime}}-v_{e_{1}}\big)\cdot\bm{\delta}\big(v_{e_{2}^{\prime}}-v_{e_{2}}\big)

where
\omega
is as above. Note that (
7.4
) forces
z_{e_{1}}=z_{e_{1}^{\prime}}
and
z_{e_{2}}=z_{e_{2}^{\prime}}
.

(3)

The associated domain
\mathcal{D}
.
The associated domain
\mathcal{D}
is defined by

(7.5)

\mathcal{D}:=\big\{\bm{t}_{\mathbb{M}}=(t_{\mathfrak{n}})\in(\mathbb{R}^{+})^{|\mathbb{M}|}:(\ell^{\prime}-1)\tau<t_{\mathfrak{n}}<\ell^{\prime}\tau\mathrm{\ if\ }\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}};\ t_{\mathfrak{n}}<t_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}\mathrm{\ if\ }\mathfrak{n}^{+}\mathrm{\ is\ parent\ of\ }\mathfrak{n}\big\}.

(4)

The associated operator
\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{M}}
.
With the associated variables
\bm{z}_{\mathcal{E}}=(z_{e}:e\in\mathcal{E})
and
\bm{t}_{\mathbb{M}}=(t_{\mathfrak{n}}:\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{M})
, we define the
associated integral operator

\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{M}}
by

(7.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{M}}(Q):=\varepsilon^{-(d-1)(|\mathcal{E}_{*}|-2|\mathbb{M}|)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d|\mathcal{E}_{*}|}\times\mathbb{R}^{|\mathbb{M}|}}\prod_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{M}}\bm{\Delta}_{\mathfrak{n}}\ Q(\bm{z}_{\mathcal{E}},\bm{t}_{\mathbb{M}})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{\mathcal{E}_{*}}\,\mathrm{d}\bm{t}_{\mathbb{M}}.

Here
\mathcal{E}_{*}
is the set of edges of
\mathbb{M}
that are
bonds or free ends
(i.e. not counting fixed ends; for full molecules
\mathbb{M}
this makes no difference). Note that in (
7.6
) we are only integrating in the variables
\bm{z}_{\mathcal{E}_{*}}
for bonds and free ends, thus
\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{M}}(Q)
is a function (or distribution) of
all fixed ends of
\mathbb{M}
.

Remark 7.4
.

We make a few remarks concerning Definition
7.3
.

(1)

The time variables
t_{\mathfrak{n}}
represent the collision or overlap time of
\mathfrak{n}
. The vector variables
z_{e}=(x_{e},v_{e})
are associated with the linear piece of trajectory along edges
e
, where
v_{e}
is the velocity (which is constant along this piece), and
x_{e}
is the position transported backwards to time
0
(so the actual position of the particle at time

t
in this linear piece is
x_{e}+tv_{e}
).

(2)

The Dirac functions in
\bm{\Delta}_{\mathfrak{n}}
prescribes the collision or overlap by forcing the elastic collision formula
in (
7.3
) and forcing the same velocity in (
7.4
). In (
7.6
), we treat all the variables
z_{e}
for
fixed ends

e
as fixed, so the result of this integral is a function (or distribution) or these fixed variables.

(3)

In (
7.5
) we have the natural (partial) time ordering, consistent with the atom ordering in Definition
3.5
: the time
t_{\mathfrak{n}}
for
\mathfrak{n}
must be before the time
t_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}
for
\mathfrak{n}^{+}
(its parent, or equivalently the next
collision). Moreover collisions in layer
\mathbb{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}
must happen in time interval
[(\ell^{\prime}-1)\tau,\ell^{\prime}\tau]
,which leads to natural orderings between different layers. These time orderings will occasionally be useful in the proof below. Apart from these, we do not assume any total ordering of collision times (except in an auxiliary step in the proof of Proposition
7.5
below).

We now state the main result of this subsection.

Proposition 7.5
.

Let
\mathbb{M}
be a full molecule (so
\mathcal{E}_{*}=\mathcal{E}
) with
r(\mathbb{M})=H
. Let
|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}}|
be the associated integral of
\mathbb{M}
defined in (
3.12
). Then, we have

(7.7)

\|\,|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}}|(\bm{z}_{H})\|_{L^{1}}=\varepsilon^{(d-1)|H|}\cdot\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{M}}(Q_{\mathbb{M}}).

where
Q_{\mathbb{M}}
is the function given by

(7.8)

Q_{\mathbb{M}}=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{D}}(\bm{t}_{\mathbb{M}})\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\varepsilon}(\bm{z}_{\mathcal{E}})\cdot\prod_{e\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{end}}^{-}}\big|f^{\mathcal{A}}\big((\ell_{1}[e]-1)\tau,x_{e}-(\ell_{1}[e]-1)\tau\cdot v_{e},v_{e}\big)\big|.

Here in (
7.8
),
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{end}}^{-}
is the set of bottom ends of
\mathbb{M}
(including empty ends, see Definition
3.6
(
1
)), and
\ell_{1}[e]
is the initial layer for bottom end
e
in Definition
3.1
(
2
). The indicator functions
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{D}}
is defined in (
7.5
), and
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\varepsilon}
is defined in (
3.9
).

Proof.

We divide the proof into 3 parts.

Proof part 1.
In this part, we make some simplifications to (
7.7
) and reduce it to (
7.10
). First we prove
|\mathbb{M}|_{p}=|\mathcal{E}|-2|\mathbb{M}|
. This is because each atom in
\mathbb{M}
has exactly
4
edges; by adding them together and noticing that each bond (between two atoms) is counted twice and each end is counted once, we obtain
4|\mathbb{M}|=2|\mathcal{E}|-|\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{end}}^{-}|-|\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{end}}^{+}|
(ignoring empty ends; however this equality remains true even with empty ends). Using also that
|\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{end}}^{-}|=|\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{end}}^{+}|=|\mathbb{M}|_{p}
(as each particle line has a unique top end and a unique bottom end, see Definition
3.1
(
2
)), we conclude that
|\mathbb{M}|_{p}=|\mathcal{E}|-2|\mathbb{M}|
, as desired.

By
|\mathbb{M}|_{p}=|\mathcal{E}|-2|\mathbb{M}|
, and note that
|H|=|\mathbb{M}|_{r}
, we see that the powers of
\varepsilon
on both sides of (
7.7
) match. Next, in the definition of
|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}}|
in (
3.12
), by integrating in the
\bm{z}_{r(\mathbb{M})}
variables, using the definition
|(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathbbm{1})_{\mathbb{M}}|
in (
3.10
) and the
L^{1}
preserving property of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathbb{M}}
, we see that

(7.9)

\big\||\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{M}}|\big\|_{L^{1}}=\varepsilon^{-(d-1)|\mathbb{M}|_{p\backslash r}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d|\mathbb{M}|_{p}}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{M}}\cdot|(|Q(\bm{z}_{p(\mathbb{M})}^{\prime})|)\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{p(\mathbb{M})}^{\prime},

where
Q
is as in (
3.13
). We may identify a particle line
{\bm{p}}\in p(\mathbb{M})
with its bottom end
e\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{end}}^{-}
, and note also that
Q_{\mathbb{M}}=|Q|\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{D}}(\bm{t}_{\mathbb{M}})
; as such, we see that (
7.7
) would follow if we can prove

(7.10)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d|\mathcal{E}\backslash\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{end}}^{-}|}\times\mathcal{D}}\prod_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{M}}\bm{\Delta}(z_{e_{1}},z_{e_{2}},z_{e_{1}^{\prime}},z_{e_{2}^{\prime}},t_{\mathfrak{n}})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}_{\mathcal{E}\backslash\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{end}}^{-}}\mathrm{d}\bm{t}_{\mathbb{M}}=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{M}}\big(\bm{z}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{end}}^{-}}\big),\quad a.e.,

where we identify
{\bm{p}}\in p(\mathbb{M})
with the bottom end
e\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{end}}^{-}
and identify
\bm{z}_{\bm{p}}^{\prime}
in (
7.9
) with
z_{e}
. (Indeed, once we prove (
7.10
), we simply integrate both sides against
\varepsilon^{-(d-1)|\mathbb{M}|_{p\backslash r}}\cdot|Q|
; the right hand side becomes (
7.9
), while the left hand side becomes
\varepsilon^{(d-1)|H|}\cdot\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{M}}(Q_{\mathbb{M}})
using the above identification, relation between
|Q|
and
Q_{\mathbb{M}}
, and the restriction
\bm{t}_{\mathbb{M}}\in\mathcal{D}
in (
7.10
).)

In the rest of this proof, we focus on proving (
7.10
).

Proof part 2.
In this part, we introduce an extension of (
7.10
) for the purpose of induction. This extension contains two extra parameters
\ell_{\mathrm{min}}
and
t_{\mathrm{min}}\in[(\ell_{\mathrm{min}}-1)\tau,\ell_{\mathrm{min}}\tau]
, and an (extra)
total ordering

\prec
for all atoms in
\mathbb{M}
, which is an extension of the partial ordering in Definition
3.5
. For each such total ordering
\prec
and each
t_{\mathrm{min}}
, define the extension of (
7.10
) as follows.

In this extension, we require that
\ell[\mathfrak{n}]\geq\ell_{\mathrm{min}}
for all atoms in
\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{M}
(we still view the lowest layer of
\mathbb{M}
as
1
). Moreover, define the new indicator function

(7.11)

\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{M},\prec,t_{\mathrm{min}}}\big(\bm{z}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{end}}^{-}}\big)

to be the same indicator function
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{M}}
, but with the additional requirements that (i) each collision and overlap happens after time
t_{\mathrm{min}}
, and (ii) if
\mathfrak{n}_{1}\prec\mathfrak{n}_{2}
in the total ordering, then the collision/overlap corresponding to
\mathfrak{n}_{1}
happens before that of
\mathfrak{n}_{2}
. Similarly, we also define the new domain

(7.12)

\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{tot}}\coloneqq\{t_{\mathbb{M}}:\,t_{\mathrm{min}}<t_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}<t_{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}\textrm{ for any }\mathfrak{n}_{1}\prec\mathfrak{n}_{2}\}.

Then, the extension is written as

(7.13)
