\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2dN}}(\mathcal{S}_{N}^{E}(t)f)(\bm{z})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2dN}}\bigg(\sum_{\bm{z}^{0}:\mathcal{H}_{N}^{E}(t)(\bm{z}^{0})=\bm{z}}f(\bm{z}^{0})\bigg)\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}=\sum_{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2dN}}\bigg(\sum_{\bm{z}^{0}\in O_{\alpha}:\mathcal{H}^{t}(\bm{z}^{0})=\bm{z}}f(\bm{z}^{0})\bigg)\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}.

The above sum in
\bm{z}^{0}
contains at most one element, and is nonzero if and only if
\bm{z}\in\mathcal{H}^{t}(O_{\alpha})
. By the above, the mapping
\mathcal{H}^{t}
is a volume preserving bijection between
O_{\alpha}
and
\mathcal{H}^{t}(O_{\alpha})
. Therefore,

(A.2)

\int\mathcal{S}_{N}^{E}(t)f(\bm{z})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}=\sum_{\alpha}\int_{\mathcal{H}^{t}(O_{\alpha})}f((\mathcal{H}^{t})^{-1}\bm{z})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}=\sum_{\alpha}\int_{O_{\alpha}}f(\bm{z}^{0})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}^{0}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2dN}}f(\bm{z}^{0})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}^{0}.

Proof of (6).
For the O-dynamics this follows from
[
15
]
; in fact the upper bound is explicit
(32N^{3/2})^{N^{2}}
. For the
(\Lambda,\Gamma)
-truncated dynamics this follows directly from (5). Consider now the E-dynamics. We know that in the E-dynamics, for each pair of particles, their distance remains
<\varepsilon
until some time when their distance becomes
\varepsilon
, and their distance remains
\geq\varepsilon
afterwards. There are at most
\binom{q}{2}
such transition times, and in any time interval
I=(t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime})
between each two consecutive transition times
t^{\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime}
, the set of particle pairs with distance
<\varepsilon
, say
K
, remains fixed.

Note that, see
[
15
]
, the O-dynamics can be viewed as the billiard motion on the billiard table

\mathcal{D}:=\bigcap_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\mathcal{D}_{ij};\quad\mathcal{D}_{ij}=\big\{\bm{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{2dN}:|x_{i}-x_{j}|\geq\varepsilon\big\}.

The proof in
[
15
]
shows that the number of boundary reflections of this billiard ball is at most
(32N^{3/2})^{N^{2}}
, by establishing some geometric properties of
\mathcal{D}
.

Now the same result in
[
15
]
still holds, if one replaces
\mathcal{D}
by
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}:=\bigcap_{(i,j)\in K^{c}}\mathcal{D}_{ij}
; indeed
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
is just the intersection of a sub-collection of the full collection of balls defining
\mathcal{D}
, and it can be checked that the same proof in
[
15
]
carries over. This then gives an upper bound on the number of collisions
in the E-dynamics within any interval
I
, because this dynamics exactly corresponds to the billiard motion on the billiard table
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
(the billiard is also restricted to the sets
\mathcal{D}_{ij}^{c}
for
(i,j)\in K
, but the boundaries of these sets are not involved in any reflections, as the billiard ball never reach the boundary of any of these sets within time interval
I
). The desired result for the E-dynamics then follows with the absolute upper bound
G_{0}(d,N)=\binom{N}{2}\cdot(32N^{3/2})^{N^{2}}
.
∎

Finally, we state and prove a result concerning the preservation of the norm
\mathrm{Bol}^{\beta}
used in Theorem
1
.

Proposition A.1
.

Recall the norm
\mathrm{Bol}^{\beta}
defined in (
1.15
). Suppose
n=n(t,x,v)
is a positive solution to the Boltzmann equation (
1.13
), and let
\beta=\beta(t)
be a fixed, strictly positive, strictly decreasing function with
\beta(0)\geq\beta>0
and
\beta^{\prime}(t)\leq-c<0
for some constant
c
. Assume
n
satisfies that

(A.3)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big\|e^{\beta(t)|v|^{2}}n(t)\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq A<\infty,

and that

(A.4)

\|n(0)\|_{\mathrm{Bol}^{\beta(0)}}+\|\nabla_{x}n(0)\|_{\mathrm{Bol}^{\beta(0)}}\leq B<\infty,

then for any
t\in[0,T]
we have

(A.5)

\|n(t)\|_{\mathrm{Bol}^{\beta(t)}}+\|\nabla_{x}n(t)\|_{\mathrm{Bol}^{\beta(t)}}\lesssim B,

where the constants in
\lesssim
may depend on
(A,\beta,c,T)
.

Proof.

First we prove (
A.5
) for
n(t)
. Define
\|n(t)\|_{\mathrm{Bol}^{\beta(t)}}:=M_{0}(t)
, by (
1.13
) we have

(A.6)

\displaystyle n(t,x,v)

\displaystyle=n(0,x-tv,v)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\big((v-v_{1})\cdot\omega\big)_{+}\cdot\big\{n(s,x-(t-s)v,v^{\prime})n(s,x-(t-s)v,v_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle-n(s,x-(t-s)v,v)n(s,x-(t-s)v,v_{1})\big\}\,\mathrm{d}\omega\mathrm{d}v_{1}\mathrm{d}s.

We focus on the Duhamel term (denote it by
n^{(1)}
). Using that
|v|^{2}+|v_{1}|^{2}=|v^{\prime}|^{2}+|v_{1}^{\prime}|^{2}
, we get that

(A.7)

\displaystyle e^{\beta(t)|v|^{2}}n^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-\beta(t)|v_{1}|^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\big((v-v_{1})\cdot\omega\big)_{+}\cdot e^{(\beta(t)-\beta(s))(|v|^{2}+|v_{1}|^{2})}

\displaystyle\times\big\{e^{\beta(s)|v^{\prime}|^{2}}n(s,x-(t-s)v,v^{\prime})\cdot e^{\beta(s)|v_{1}^{\prime}|^{2}}n(s,x-(t-s)v,v_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle-e^{\beta(s)|v|^{2}}n(s,x-(t-s)v,v)\cdot e^{\beta(s)|v_{1}|^{2}}n(s,x-(t-s)v,v_{1})\big\}\,\mathrm{d}\omega\mathrm{d}v_{1}\mathrm{d}s.

Note that
\beta(t)\geq\beta
and
\beta(t)-\beta(s)\leq-c(t-s)
, it is easy to see that

\big((v-v_{1})\cdot\omega\big)_{+}\cdot e^{(\beta(t)-\beta(s))(|v|^{2}+|v_{1}|^{2})/2}\lesssim_{\beta,c}(t-s)^{-1/2}.

Let
K\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}
be such that
(t-s)\max(|v|,|v_{1}|)\sim(t-s)\max(|v^{\prime}|,|v_{1}^{\prime}|)\sim K
(with
\sim
replaced by
\lesssim
if
K=1
), then from (
A.7
) we have

(A.8)

\displaystyle e^{\beta(t)|v|^{2}}n^{(1)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta,c,T}\sum_{K}e^{-\theta K^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-1/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-\beta|v_{1}|^{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{(t-s)\max(|v|,|v_{1}|)\lesssim K}

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\big\{e^{\beta(s)|v^{\prime}|^{2}}n(s,x-(t-s)v,v^{\prime})\cdot e^{\beta(s)|v_{1}^{\prime}|^{2}}n(s,x-(t-s)v,v_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle+e^{\beta(s)|v|^{2}}n(s,x-(t-s)v,v)\cdot e^{\beta(s)|v_{1}|^{2}}n(s,x-(t-s)v,v_{1})\big\}\,\mathrm{d}\omega\mathrm{d}v_{1}\mathrm{d}s,

where
\theta\gtrsim_{\beta,cT}1
is a positive constant. In (
A.8
), if
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
is fixed and
|x-k|\leq 1
, then for
y=x-(t-s)v
we have
|y-k|\lesssim K
, therefore we have

(A.9)

e^{\beta(s)|v^{\prime}|^{2}}n(s,x-(t-s)v,v^{\prime})\leq\sum_{k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},|k^{\prime}-k|\lesssim K}G_{k^{\prime}}(s),

for some
G_{k^{\prime}}(s)
satisfying
\sum_{k^{\prime}}G_{k^{\prime}}(s)\lesssim\|n(s)\|_{\mathrm{Bol}^{\beta(s)}}\leq M_{0}(s)
. The other three terms satisfy the same estimate; moreover, by (
A.3
) we also have
e^{\beta(s)|v^{\prime}|^{2}}n(s,x-(t-s)v,v^{\prime})\leq A
and the same for the other three terms. In summary, for every
|x-k|\leq 1
and
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

(A.10)

\displaystyle e^{\beta(t)|v|^{2}}n^{(1)}(x,v)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta,c,T}\sum_{K}e^{-\theta K^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-1/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-\beta|v_{1}|^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}A\cdot\sum_{|k^{\prime}-k|\leq K}G_{k^{\prime}}(s)\,\mathrm{d}\omega\mathrm{d}v_{1}\mathrm{d}s.

Upon taking supremum in
(x,v)
and summing in
k
and
K
, and doing the same for the transport term
n(0,x-tv,v)
, we obtain that

M_{0}(t)\lesssim_{A,\beta,c,T}B+\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-1/2}M_{0}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s.

By Gronwall, this implies
M_{0}(t)\lesssim_{A,\beta,c,T}B
for any
t\in[0,T]
. Finally, for the proof of the
\nabla_{x}
derivative, just notice that, by (
1.13
) we have
(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})(\nabla_{x}n)=\mathcal{Q}(n,\nabla_{x}n)+\mathcal{Q}(\nabla_{x}n,n)
, so the same result holds by repeating the proof above.
∎
